{"step_index":89,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-13T02:11:11Z","content":"All 51 tests pass. Here's a summary of the changes:\n\n## Summary\n\n**The problem**: When comments in the social feed reply to each other, the responses were generic — they used `generateDynamicComment(message:)` which generates a reaction to the *post topic* regardless of what the comment being replied to actually said. For example, someone saying \"Incredible! :P\" might get a reply about \"your grid\" or a competitive taunt, instead of a natural \"Thanks!\" response.\n\n**The fix**: Added a new `generateContextualReply(to:message:)` function that analyzes the actual comment text being replied to and detects its tone/intent:\n\n| Detected Tone | Example Comment | Response Style |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Friend request** | \"Can I add you? 🤝\" | \"Sure! My code is in my profile\" |\n| **Question** | \"How long did that take?\" | Topic-aware answers (e.g. HoF, streak, speed tips) |\n| **Competitive** | \"You won't be ahead for long\" | \"Bring it on 😏\", \"Talk is cheap — show me the screenshot\" |\n<truncated 449 bytes>"}

{"step_index":135,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-13T02:16:37Z","content":"All 51 tests pass. Here's a summary:\n\n## Summary\n\nUpdated `searchFriends` in `MockSocialService` with two fixes:\n\n1. **Names come from the leaderboard only** — The pool is built from `leaderboardGamertags` (150+ gamertags like `NeonRacer607539`, `CosmicDust555331`) and `leaderboardRealNames` + `leaderboardLastNames` combos (like `Thierry Al-Rashid`). No random or invented names are generated.\n\n2. **Friend codes use `XXX-YYY` format** — Matching the profile system (`QNP-LQR` style) instead of the old `XXX-2244` stub. Codes are derived deterministically from the player name so the same player always shows the same code.\n\nAdditionally, when a **search query** is provided, the full leaderboard pool is searched (not just a random sample), so searching for a name like \"Neon\" will always find `NeonRacer607539`. The 20-item limit only applies to the empty-query browse mode."}

{"step_index":41,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-06T22:21:40Z","content":"I've fixed the issue! The discrepancy was caused by `FeedCommentsView` hardcoding exactly two comments regardless of the post, even though the mock data for the first post had a `commentCount` of 3.\n\nHere's what I did to resolve it:\n1. **Updated the Data Model (`ParityModels.swift`)**:\n   - Added a `comments: [String]` array property to the `SocialFeedItem` struct to hold the actual comment texts rather than just a count.\n   - Updated the mock data in `MockSocialService` to provide the appropriate comments. For the first item (\"Sam\"), it now provides three actual comments: `\"Nice run!\"`, `\"That milestone path is clean.\"`, and `\"Amazing strategy!\"` to match its `commentCount` of 3.\n2. **Updated the UI (`DuolingoParityScreens.swift`)**:\n   - Modified `FeedCommentsView` to iterate over `item.comments` using a `ForEach` loop, dynamically displaying the comments attached to each specific `SocialFeedItem` instead of static hardcoded text blocks. \n\n<truncated 123 bytes>"}

{"step_index":92,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-07T00:20:25Z","content":"I've updated the comment system to fully support your requests!\n\nHere is what I've implemented:\n1. **Comment Model Updates**: I created a new `SocialFeedComment` structure to hold the author's name, the text, and a `createdAt: Date` timestamp. `SocialFeedItem` was updated to store an array of these comment objects instead of raw strings. \n2. **Dynamic Time Formatting**: I implemented a `formatDate(_:)` helper function inside `FeedCommentsView`. It uses a `RelativeDateTimeFormatter` to elegantly display relative dates/times (e.g., \"1 hour ago\", \"just now\"). \n3. **Interactive Commenting**: The `FeedCommentsView` now uses `@State` for the `SocialFeedItem`, allowing the view to mutate when a new comment is added. When you type in the text field and hit submit, it instantly appends the comment to the list with the current date and increments the comment count.\n\n<truncated 186 bytes>"}

{"step_index":185,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-07T00:31:40Z","content":"I have implemented the `generateDynamicName()` function and transformed your social mock data into a bustling, dynamic live event!\n\nHere is what I've done to make it feel like a massive live feed:\n1. **Millions of Leaderboard Combinations:** I implemented the dynamic name generator logic directly inside `MockSocialService`. It perfectly matches the leaderboard's logic: \n   * **25% Real Names:** Combines diverse real first names (James, Miguel, Sophia, etc.) with real last names (Smith, Garcia, etc.).\n   * **75% Gamertags:** Selects from over 100+ base gamertags (e.g., NeonRacer, TokyoTiger) and appends a randomized 6-digit number, resulting in tens of millions of possible combinations.\n2. **Making it an Event:** I overhauled the `feed()` function. Instead of hardcoding 3 static posts, it now procedurally generates **25 unique social events** every time it's called! \n   * Each event uses a random dynamic name, random milestone achievement (16K, 1M, 8M, etc.), and random engagement metrics.\n<truncated 522 bytes>"}

{"step_index":197,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-07T09:32:04Z","content":"I've overhauled the comment generation system so you'll rarely see the same message twice!\n\nInstead of pulling from a small, fixed list of 8 options, I created a robust `generateDynamicComment()` method that algorithmically constructs comments. It works by mixing and matching various elements to create natural-sounding player reactions, specifically tailored to the game.\n\nIt pieces together over **4,000 unique combinations** using:\n*   **Openers:** (\"Omg,\", \"Honestly,\", \"Yoo,\")\n*   **Subjects:** (\"that run\", \"your board\", \"this setup\")\n*   **Reactions & Adjectives:** (\"is mind-blowing\", \"looks so clean\", \"Incredible!\")\n*   **Questions:** (\"How long did that take?\", \"Any tips for this tier?\")\n*   **Emojis:** Dynamically injected (🔥, 🤯, 🚀, 👀)\n\nNow, your generated social feed events are populated with highly varied, organic-feeling comment threads like *\"Honestly, that score is next level 🤯\"* or *\"GG! What's your secret?\"*. The feed will finally look like a bustling, diverse community!"}

{"step_index":259,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-07T20:41:00Z","content":"That's a great observation, and it makes perfect sense. I've completely overhauled the symbol injection so that it's now context-aware!\n\nHere’s how the logic works now:\n1. **Tone Mapping**: The generator evaluates the tone of the comment before appending any extra characters.\n2. **Positive/Excited Comments** (e.g., \"Wow, that run is insane!\"): Get matched with symbols like `xD`, `:D`, `:)`, `🔥`, `🚀`, `!!`, or `>:)`\n3. **Questions** (e.g., \"What's your secret?\"): Specifically trigger inquisitive symbols like `?!`, `...`, `🤔`, or `👀`\n4. **Jealous/Frustrated Comments** (e.g., \"So jealous\", \"I always lose right here\"): Safely draw from your newly requested sad/frustrated emoticons like `:(`, `:((`, `>:(`, `:/`, `;-(`, or `😭`.\n5. **Typo Simulation**: I also left a small 10% chance for a single raw keyboard symbol (like `%` or `^`) to drop in at the end, simulating a quick typing error to keep things authentic.\n\n<truncated 98 bytes>"}

{"step_index":271,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-05-07T20:43:10Z","content":"I've added a brand new **Competitive** tone category to the comment generator!\n\nNow, players in the social feed will occasionally engage in some healthy rivalry. This new category generates comments like:\n*   \"I am going to reach higher milestones than you!\"\n*   \"Watch your back, I'm catching up.\"\n*   \"Enjoy it while it lasts.\"\n*   \"My next run will beat that.\"\n*   \"I'm coming for your spot.\"\n\nThese competitive comments are also strictly mapped to their own set of context-aware symbols and emojis, so they will naturally end with things like `>:)`, `😏`, `😈`, `⚔️`, `🎯`, `🏁`, or `💨`. \n\nThe social feed now covers everything from supportive congratulations and curious questions to pure competitive banter!"}

The problem is in `GameStore.addBonusSpins()` on line 3734-3738:\n\n```swift\nprivate func addBonusSpins(_ amount: Int) {\n    guard amount > 0 else { return }\n    let spinState = SpinWheelState()   // ← Creates a NEW instance!\n    spinState.addBonusSpins(amount)\n}\n```\n\nIt creates a **brand new** `SpinWheelState()` every time, which reads the current bonus spins from UserDefaults, adds to it, and saves back. But the *existing* `SpinWheelState` instance that the `SpinWheelView` is observing never gets updated — it still has the old `bonusSpins` value in memory. Since `SpinWheelState` is `@Observable`, the view only sees changes on the instance it's watching.\n\nThe same issue exists for `addMultipliers` on line 3740-3744.\n\nThe real fix: `GameStore` should use the **shared** `SpinWheelState` from the environment instead of creating a new one. {"step_index":223,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-03-29T20:17:40Z","content":"Now I have the complete picture. The `DailyQuestStore.claim()` calls `onReward?(rewards)` (line 200), which invokes the app-level `applyRewards` closure that correctly uses the shared `spinWheelState`. So daily quest spin rewards **should** work.\n\nBut wait — looking at line 217-219 of the app:\n```swift\ndailyQuestStore.onReward = { rewards in\n    homeState.addGems(0) // trigger UI refresh\n}\n```\n\nThe daily quest `onReward` is **overwritten** with a closure that only refreshes gems! {"step_index":278,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-03-31T16:18:49Z","content":"I see the issue. Our earlier fix stamps the slot when using a bonus spin **only if `lastConsumedSlot` is `nil`** (line 139). But if the player has already consumed a slot in a previous 4-hour window, `lastConsumedSlot` is set to that old slot. {"step_index":281,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-03-31T16:19:06Z","content":"Fixed! {"step_index":245,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-03-30T18:39:36Z","content":"Now I see the critical issue! Let me think about why this might break...\n\nActually, wait — the real critical insight is this: `AudioServiceKey` stores `any AudioServiceProtocol`. SwiftUI's environment key storage uses `==` comparison to determine if a value has changed. But `any AudioServiceProtocol` does NOT conform to `Equatable`. {"step_index":132,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-03-21T03:11:50Z","content":"Fixed! The timer now freezes at the actual remaining time when the challenge ends, instead of snapping to `0:00`. Here's what changed in `CustomChallengeGameScreen.swift`:\n\n1. **Added `frozenTimeRemaining`** — a `@State` var that captures the time when the challenge ends\n2. **`timeRemainingAt`** — returns the frozen value if set, instead of unconditionally returning `0` when `challengeEnded` is true\n3. **`endChallenge`** — captures `frozenTimeRemaining = timeRemaining` before setting `challengeEnded = true`\n\nSo now when you win, the timer will show whatever time you had left (e.g., `1:23`) rather than `0:00`."}

{"step_index":139,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-03-21T03:14:34Z","content":"Found it. Lines 1037-1042 in `performCommit`:\n\n```swift\nlet savedGems = UserDefaults.standard.integer(forKey: \"coins\")\nlet baseGems = savedGems > 0 ? savedGems : previousGems\nstate.gems = baseGems + max(0, gemsEarned)\n```\n\nEvery time a merge happens, it reads the **main game's** gem count from UserDefaults and resets the challenge's gems to that value. So if you spent 1,000 gems on power-ups in the challenge, the next merge resets your gems back to the full main-game amount — effectively refunding everything.\n\nThe fix is to skip this UserDefaults-based gem correction in sandboxed mode.","tool_calls":[{"name":"task_boundary","args":{"Mode":"\"EXECUTION\"","PredictedTaskSize":"3","TaskName":"\"Fixing Challenge Mode Gem Refund Bug\"","TaskStatus":"\"Applying fix to skip UserDefaults gem correction in sandboxed mode\"","TaskSummary":"\"Found root cause: `performCommit()` reads the main game's gem count from UserDefaults on every merge and resets the challenge's gems to that value, refunding any spending.\""}},{"name":"replace_file_content","args":{"AllowMultiple":"false","CodeMarkdownLanguage":"\"swift\"","Complexity":"7","Description":"\"Fix gem refund bug in challenge mode: sandboxed games now use their own gem state instead of reading the main game's gems from UserDefaults on every merge. This was causing spent gems to be immediately refunded.\"","EndLine":"1042","Instruction":"\"In sandboxed mode, use the challenge's own gem state (previousGems) as the base instead of reading from UserDefaults. The UserDefaults correction is only needed for the main game to prevent race conditions between engine state and persisted state.\"","ReplacementContent":"\"        // Update state but DO NOT schedule refill reveal yet, as refill hasn't happened\\n        // Track gems earned from this action (gifts, etc.)\\n        let previousGems = state.gems\\n        let gemsEarned = newState.gems - state.gems  // Gems awar\n<truncated 601 bytes>","StartLine":"1035","TargetContent":"\"        // Update state but DO NOT schedule refill reveal yet, as refill hasn't happened\\n        // Track gems earned from this action (gifts, etc.)\\n        let previousGems = state.gems\\n        let savedGems = UserDefaults.standard.integer(forKey: \\\"c\n<truncated 270 bytes>","TargetFile":"\"/Users/angelhendersonjr/Development/2244/Packages/GameApp/Sources/GameApp/GameStore.swift\""}}]}
{"step_index":143,"source":"MODEL","type":"PLANNER_RESPONSE","status":"DONE","created_at":"2026-03-21T03:14:55Z","content":"Fixed. Here's what was happening:\n\n**The bug:** Every time you merged tiles in challenge mode, `performCommit()` read `UserDefaults.standard.integer(forKey: \"coins\")` — which is your **main game's** gem balance (e.g., 50,000). It then reset the challenge's gem count to that value plus any newly earned gems. So any gems you spent on power-ups were immediately restored on the next merge.\n\n**The fix:** In sandboxed mode, the gem calculation now uses `previousGems` (the challenge's own gem state) as the base instead of reading from UserDefaults. The UserDefaults correction is preserved for the main game where it's needed to prevent race conditions.\n\nThis is the same class of bug as the 262K/65K issue — sandboxed challenge mode was leaking state from the main game via UserDefaults."}
